KNOCKTURNAL: ELSEWHERED

PILOT — CANON VERIFICATION COPY

⸻

PART 1

The first sign was not thunder.

Thunder had the decency to come from somewhere.

This sound came from everywhere at once—a deep pressure rolling beneath Runeterra, too low to belong to the sky and too broad to belong to the earth. Mountains caught it. Rivers carried it. The sea shivered against coastlines that had not moved.

Then the horizon blinked.

Not flashed.

Blinking would be the closest honest word for it.

For one fraction of a second, pieces of the world were simply elsewhere.

A distant ridgeline vanished behind the side of a building that had never existed there. An unfamiliar cloud layer hung beneath Runeterra’s own. Metal roads appeared where no roads had been laid, their severed ends biting directly into stone and sand.

Then everything held.

Mostly.

Aye, and mostly is a dreadful word when you’re speaking of reality.

Far from any person with the luxury of understanding it, an entire piece of another world had arrived.

The Avengers Compound came with walls, laboratories, wreckable concrete, electrical systems, furniture, frightened human beings—and Bruce Banner with six Infinity Stones burning on his right arm.

There was no grand announcement.

No portal opened politely beneath them.

One instant, Banner was forcing the Nano Gauntlet over his hand.

The next, outside the compound, New York was gone.

Nobody in the room had time to process that.

The Stones lit.

Banner screamed.

Thor started toward him.

Steve stopped him.

Tony watched the energy crawl across Banner’s body with the expression of a man watching a calculation become either salvation or murder.

The compound lights flickered.

Beyond the walls, Runeterra waited under an unfamiliar sky.

Banner forced himself upright.

His fingers closed.

And snapped.

For the people inside that room, the sound was almost disappointingly small.

For Runeterra, it was not.

White light detonated through the compound.

Gamma radiation followed.

Energy from six Stones rolled outward—and struck the first place where the world no longer agreed with itself.

The wave bent.

Not by intention.

Not toward anyone.

It simply met a wound in geography that should not have been there, and part of the blast vanished into it.

[Author-removed historical passage omitted.]

Elsewhere, another piece of that same impossible pulse found water.

Catfish Billy felt it first through six barbels.

The huge creature stopped dead beneath the surface.

His body had been moving with the effortless zero-drag propulsion of something that belonged in water far more completely than anything his size had a right to. Then every barbel stiffened.

Inside him, Chris was elbow-deep in somebody else’s pockets.

Not literally.

On the television, Arthur Morgan crouched over a freshly dead outlaw while Chris hammered the loot command like the corpse had personally insulted him.

“Hold on, hold on, hold the fuck on. I ain’t leaving yet.”

Another item entered the satchel.

Chris nodded.

“See? Exactly. You check everybody.”

Billy stopped moving.

The lounge tilted slightly.

Chris paused.

He looked at the screen.

Looked at the Pepsi refrigerator.

Looked around.

“Billy.”

No answer.

The banjo on Billy’s exterior carried Chris’s voice into the water as a muffled vibration.

“Billy, what the fuck are you doing?”

Then the Snap reached him.

The stomach lounge convulsed.

Chris fell sideways off the couch.

The television remained standing.

The couch did not.

The refrigerator slid six feet, stopped, and somehow stayed plugged into absolutely nothing Chris had ever understood.

“OH, COME ON!”

The walls pulled away.

That was the disturbing part.

They did not break.

They receded.

The fleshy cavern surrounding Chris stretched outward until distance itself stopped behaving like the inside of a creature. New organic passages opened behind membranes. Other spaces pressed outward beyond them—separated, sealed, visible in places through translucent living walls.

The lounge remained intact at the center of it.

But it was no longer the whole place.

It was a home inside something much larger.

Chris slowly climbed to his feet.

A Pepsi can rolled past.

He snatched it before it could disappear into a newly opened passage.

Then another.

Then a controller.

Then a loose cable.

“Fuck that. That’s mine.”

He stuffed the cable under one arm, Pepsi under the other, controller against his chest, eyes moving constantly as the organic space kept unfolding.

Billy’s voice did not come from the walls.

Billy did not speak like that.

Instead Chris felt the meaning through the bond.

Confusion.

Pain.

Expansion.

Chris stared down one long, pulsing corridor that absolutely had not existed ten seconds earlier.

“Oh, you gotta be fucken kidding me.”

The living membrane at its far end cleared.

Beyond it lay another space.

And another beyond that.

Chris’s face changed.

Not fear yet.

Landlord horror.

“BILLY.”

Outside, Catfish Billy erupted from the water.

Hundreds of pounds of slick mottled brute launched upward, hat still planted on his massive head, barbels lashing as water rolled from his body.

He hit the surface again hard enough to throw a real wall of water outward.

Then he floated there.

Still.

Feeling the new depth inside himself.

Chris’s voice blasted from the banjo.

“WHY DO YOU HAVE MORE ROOMS?”

Billy blinked.

Chris looked back at his television.

Arthur was still crouched beside the corpse.

The game had not paused.

“Aw, fuck.”

Chris lunged for the controller.

The stomach-space had just transformed into something impossible, and he still looted the second body before investigating it.

Priorities are revealing things.

Far away from both of them, Kaze stood on the dark rise, lean beneath scorched asymmetrical armor, chest open to the strange wind. Heavy dreadlocks shifted around his face as his white eyes followed the impossible horizon. One hand settled against The Oblivion Tempest while the seam moved again.

Across the distance, a wall of cloud ended in a perfectly vertical line.

Beyond that line was another sky.

Kaze stared.

“…What the fuck?”

He crouched and touched the stone beneath his Air Force.

Solid.

The air smelled normal.

The wind did not.

Yasuo’s learned wind sense moved through the current and returned with contradictions.

Heat from one direction.

Cold from another.

Metal somewhere that should have been wilderness.

Smoke that did not belong to anything he knew was burning.

Kaze stood.

The seam ahead shifted three feet sideways without moving through the ground between those positions.

He watched that carefully.

Then something small dropped from it.

A piece of concrete.

It struck the slope.

Rebar protruded from one side.

He crouched.

Touched it.

Ordinary material.

No magic he recognized.

No obvious trick.

He looked back at the impossible horizon.

A long pause.

Then the corner of his mouth moved.

Not a smile.

Interest.

“Okay.”

Another tremor rolled beneath his shoes.

Much stronger.

Kaze straightened.

“Now this shit got my attention.”

He stepped off the ridge.

His legs drove him upward with enough force to fracture the stone beneath him.

He crossed open air in a ballistic arc, caught another surface farther down, redirected, and launched again.

Toward the contradiction.

Across Runeterra, the same event was being discovered in different ways.

Ships found stars where familiar routes expected none.

Roads that had been reliable for generations reached places they had never touched.

A Runeterran trade path met a strip of foreign asphalt and simply stopped.

Water flowed toward slopes that had not been present the previous hour.

Animals refused paths before people understood why.

Messengers began carrying reports that contradicted one another because every report was true where it had been written.

One traveler swore a metal fortress had appeared beyond the desert.

Another insisted the desert itself had vanished behind towers of glass.

Neither knew the other existed.

Neither knew about Banner.


Neither knew the enormous catfish in the water now contained an expanding internal territory and one furious barefoot chef still looting digital corpses.

And none of them knew what Kaze was moving toward.

The people inside the Avengers Compound were only beginning to realize the problem themselves.

Banner lay breathing hard.

Tony looked toward a window.

His eyes narrowed.

That was not their skyline.

Steve followed his gaze.

Thor turned.

Rocket climbed onto a better angle and stared outside.

Nobody spoke for a moment.

A desert stretched beyond the compound.

Not New York.

Not anything they recognized.

Then Clint’s phone rang.

His entire face changed.

Proof that Banner’s Snap had done what they needed.

For perhaps three seconds, relief existed.

Then Nebula looked upward.

Her expression tightened.

Above the compound, something enormous broke through the cloud.

A warship.

Sanctuary II.

Thanos had arrived with them.

Inside CATFISH BAYOU, Chris had finally paused the game.


And Kaze, still bounding toward a horizon that did not belong to his world, saw a black shape spreading across the distant sky.

He landed hard enough to buckle stone.

Looked up.

Watched the first missiles begin to fall.

Kaze squinted.

“…Aight.”

A beat.

“Somebody’s fucken shooting.”

The bombardment struck.

And the Avengers Compound disappeared beneath fire.

⸻

PART 2

The bombardment did not merely destroy the Avengers Compound.

It drove it into Runeterra.

Concrete folded downward beneath Sanctuary II’s guns. Steel support beams buckled. Whole sections of the foreign structure dropped into earth that had never been poured for it, forcing stone and sand outward as if Runeterra itself were trying to reject the weight.

The first barrage ended.

Then gravity finished the job.

Floors collapsed into floors.

Water burst through broken infrastructure.

Smoke climbed into a sky where none of those buildings belonged.

Beneath the ruins, Clint Barton woke with the Nano Gauntlet close enough to touch and an army already coming to take it from him.

Bruce Banner woke pinned beneath debris with one arm ruined.

Rocket and Rhodey found themselves trapped in rising water.

Scott Lang vanished into the wreckage and began doing what Scott Lang tended to do when reality became unreasonable: surviving it anyway.

And above them, three men climbed from the destruction to meet Thanos.

Aye, there are moments where history appears to hold still.

This was not one.

Tony Stark attacked first because waiting had already cost him five years.

Thor came in carrying both Stormbreaker and Mjolnir, rage sharpened by failure rather than dulled by it.

Steve Rogers followed because Steve Rogers would have followed if the thing waiting beyond the smoke had been death itself.

Thanos met all three.

The battle that followed belonged to them.

For a while.

Hammer against blade.

Lightning through smoke.

Repulsors against armor.

Stormbreaker carving sparks from a weapon built to survive gods.

No one paused to ask why the distant horizon now contained mountains that did not exist on Earth.

No one had enough spare attention to care.

Thanos drove them backward.

Tony hit the ground.

Thor lost Mjolnir.

Steve caught it.

Even Mimir, teller of tales though I am, must confess there are some images that know perfectly well what they are.

Lightning came down.

Steve Rogers stood with the hammer of Thor in one hand and his broken shield in the other.

Thanos learned very quickly that worthiness and restraint are not the same thing.

Far beyond the battle, something else was learning its body.


There was too much of him now.

The first few strides were ugly—not weak, never that, but inefficient. Each footfall wanted to bury itself. Each acceleration wanted to tear the ground backward. His new center of mass sat differently. His shoulders carried enough weight to change how the rest of him moved.


One stride.

Correction.

Another.

Less wasted force.


“Stay close.”


The dog did not slow.


“Yeah. Figured.”

The sky flashed ahead.

Not lightning.

Weapons.

A burning object came over the ridge at an angle neither of them expected.


His stance changed before thought had time to become language.

One foot dug in.

Shoulder turned.


The piece of alien wreckage struck his gauntlet.


He redirected.


Only half.

He stared at the smoking trench.

Then at his hand.

“Huh.”


“I know.”


“That’s what I’m saying.”

They continued.

The new body frightened him less now.

What frightened him was how quickly it was beginning to make sense.

Elsewhere, Catfish Billy had discovered that his insides now possessed neighborhoods.

Chris had discovered something worse.

Storage potential.

The first panic had lasted perhaps two minutes.

Then the chef brain took over.

He dragged the couch back into position.

Recovered every Pepsi he could find.

Checked the console.

Checked the television.

Checked the controller twice.

Then began walking.

One of the new corridors curved beyond his old lounge in a way that should have brought him directly into Billy’s flesh.

Instead it continued.

The wall pulsed softly beside him.

Chris stopped.

Tapped it.

“That is fucken disgusting.”

The wall pulsed again.

“Don’t do that.”

It did it anyway.

Chris kept walking.

The first new chamber was empty.

The second was larger.

The third separated itself from him behind a translucent membrane.

Chris could see through it perfectly.

He stepped close.

Touched the surface.

Solid.

He leaned his face nearer.

“Okay.”

He stepped back.

Thought about it.

Then pointed at the wall like Billy might somehow see the gesture from outside.

“If you’re gonna keep eating weird shit, they’re going in there.”

Billy’s amusement rolled through the bond.

Chris narrowed his eyes.

“I’m serious.”

More amusement.

“Billy.”

Outside, Billy swam faster.

Chris sighed.

Then he noticed a loose screw near the edge of the old lounge.

He picked it up.

Inspected it.

Pocketed it.

One apocalypse at a time.

Above the Avengers Compound, Thanos finally broke Steve’s shield.

Half of it fell away.

Steve fell with it.

Across the battlefield, Thanos’s army waited.

Chitauri.

Outriders.

Leviathans.

Armored ranks stretching behind him beneath the impossible Runeterran sky.

Steve pulled himself upright.

Alone.

Then a voice came through his comm.

A portal opened.

Then another.

And another.

The Battle of Earth stopped being a last stand.

It became a war.

Wakandans crossed first.

Sorcerers.

Asgardians.

Ravagers.

The restored.

The returned.

People Thanos had erased stepped into a world he had never seen.

The portals did not transport them back to Earth.

Earth was not there.

They emerged into Runeterra because that was where the battlefield now existed.

Beyond the golden circles, familiar places remained visible for moments at a time.

Wakanda.

New Asgard.

Titan.

Kamar-Taj routes and gathering points.

Then each fighter stepped through and found alien soil underfoot.

That mattered.

Not emotionally.

Physically.

Some landed on shattered concrete.

Others on stone.

Others on loose sand thrown across the battlefield by the compound’s impact.

Formations corrected.

Mounts adjusted.

Sorcerers looked beyond their own portals and saw a horizon none of their spells had created.

There was no time to investigate.

Steve raised Mjolnir.

The armies moved.

The collision could be heard for miles.

Kaze heard it before he saw it clearly.

Not one fight.

Thousands.

Metal.

Gunfire.

Animal screams.

Energy weapons.

Human voices.

Something huge roaring overhead.

His next landing destroyed the upper face of a stone outcrop.

He dropped into a crouch as fragments rolled beneath his Air Forces.

The noise ahead made his head lift.

“Okay.”

He stood.

“That’s a lotta motherfuckers.”

The wind carried smoke across him.

Kaze tasted blood in the air without needing any special sense to identify it.

A shape swept overhead.

One of Thanos’s troop carriers.

Another followed.

Kaze watched both.

Then looked down.

The ground beneath him had changed.

The rock ended at a line.

Not cracked.

Ended.

On the other side lay pulverized concrete and churned battlefield mud.

Kaze stepped across it.

Nothing happened.

He looked back.

Stone.

Concrete.

Two pieces of two worlds touching badly.

“That’s weird.”

A screech interrupted him.

Three Outriders came over the wreckage.

Fast.

Low.

No hesitation.

They saw a lone figure entering the battlefield and behaved as Thanos’s army had been trained to behave.

Remove it.

Kaze stopped walking.

The first Outrider jumped.

He caught it by the face.

Its momentum ended so violently the body whipped around his arm.

Kaze looked at it.

The creature clawed at his wrist.

He glanced at the claws.

Then at its face.

“Hm.”

His fingers closed.

The skull collapsed.

The other two reached him together.

Kaze turned with the first corpse still in his hand and used it as a club.

The impact folded one Outrider sideways into the second.

Both hit the ground.

Kaze dropped the corpse.

One tried to rise.

His foot came down.

The body disappeared into the broken concrete beneath him.

Kaze stared at the crater for a moment.

“Squishy.”

Then something blocked the light.

A Leviathan passed overhead.

Kaze leaned back and followed it with his eyes.

Now that interested him.

The creature-bank-ship hybrid curved through the battle, carrying Chitauri along armored ridges.

Kaze watched its movement.

Weight.

Armor.

Speed.

Trajectory.

His body lowered.

Then he launched.

The ground exploded behind him.

He crossed the distance like a fired round.

The Leviathan never reacted.

Kaze struck its flank feet-first.

Armor cratered inward.

The entire creature lurched sideways.

Chitauri were thrown from its back.

Kaze planted one hand into the damaged plating, tore himself through the opening he had made, and vanished inside.

For approximately two seconds, the Leviathan continued flying.

Then The Oblivion Tempest came through its underside.

A cut opened along the belly.

Then another.

Then something inside gave way.

The creature folded.

Kaze kicked free as it descended.

The Leviathan crashed through Thanos’s advancing formation, killing soldiers beneath its own mass and plowing a trench through the field.

Kaze landed on its corpse.

He looked down at the opened armor.

“Better.”

That got attention.

Not everyone.

There were too many battles for that.

But enough.

A Wakandan fighter saw him.

Two Chitauri gunners saw him.

Rocket saw the Leviathan drop and immediately looked for what had killed it.

Peter Quill looked too late.

Sam Wilson caught the end of Kaze’s landing while banking away from incoming fire.

None of them knew who he was.

None received an answer for free.

The Outriders did not care.

More came.

Kaze sighed.

“Y’all don’t learn?”

They charged.

He drew The Oblivion Tempest.

The first swing was barely theatrical enough to deserve the word.

The blade crossed once.

Wind followed it.

Not natural wind.

Learned wind.

Controlled.

Infected by everything Kaze had earned the right to put through it.

The front rank came apart.

The pressure continued beyond them.

Alien bodies lifted.

Armor opened.

A line appeared through wreckage thirty yards behind them.

Then the delayed force hit.

The battlefield erupted sideways.

Kaze watched the damage travel.

His eyes sharpened slightly.

“Okay.”

He had learned something.

The next wave hesitated.

That was also information.

Kaze rested the sword loosely against one shoulder.

“See? Now y’all thinking.”

A blast came from his right.

He moved his head.

The shot missed by inches.

Kaze looked toward the Chitauri weapon that had fired it.

The gunner fired again.

Kaze disappeared from where the gunner had aimed.

One instant he was beside the Leviathan.

The next his Air Force was on the gunner’s chest.

The impact drove alien and machinery through the wreck beneath them.

Kaze remained standing when the dust settled.

He looked toward the center of the war.

That was when he noticed the Gauntlet.

It moved through hands.

Clint.

T’Challa.

Across wreckage.

Through a battlefield where entire armies were trying to reach one object.

Kaze’s expression changed.

Not because he knew what it was.

Because everyone else did.

That was often more useful.

He watched T’Challa run.

Thanos saw him too.

So did half the battlefield.

Kaze’s attention settled.

“Why everybody chasing that?”

No one answered.

A figure slammed into the earth nearby.


Not gracefully.


His eyes found Kaze.

Kaze looked at him.

There was a pause.


Thirteen feet tall.


Kaze stared.


Kaze pointed at him.

“The fuck happened to you?”


Then back at Kaze.

“Light.”

Kaze waited.


“Bad light.”

Kaze’s mouth twitched.

“Yeah, no shit.”


Kaze looked down.


A Chitauri craft screamed toward them.


Not because Kaze had failed to notice it.


One blast scraped the reinforced gauntlet.

Short hook.

The punch hit the craft beneath its forward hull.


The craft went up instead of back.

Kaze followed it with his eyes.

“Okay.”


“What?”

“You got bigger and somehow learned not to hit everything.”


“I already knew how to fight.”

“Yeah, yeah.”

Kaze looked back toward the moving Gauntlet.


“What is that?”

“Don’t know.”

“Then why you looking at it?”

“Because apparently every motherfucker here wants it.”


Fair.

The battlefield suddenly darkened.

Sanctuary II changed targets.

Its guns rotated toward the armies below.

Thanos had decided that if his soldiers could not control the field, the field itself could die with them.

The first batteries fired.


Kaze didn’t.

Not yet.

He looked up.

Tracked the shells.

Calculated.

Then a streak of light entered the atmosphere.

Carol Danvers came through Sanctuary II.

Not around it.

Through it.

Armor peeled apart behind her.

Sections of the ship exploded in sequence as she crossed its length, turned, and ripped through the warship again.

Kaze’s head slowly followed her path.

For the first time since arriving, he smiled properly.

“Who the fuck is that?”


“Don’t know.”

Kaze’s smile widened.

“She look fun.”

Then the ground moved.

Not shook.

Moved.

The battlefield seam behind them slid sideways.

A whole section of Avengers rubble vanished from alignment with the crater where it had been.

Runeterran stone replaced part of the gap.

One of the golden sorcerer portals flickered—not because the spell failed, but because the ground on one side of it was suddenly no longer adjacent to what had been there a moment before.

Combatants stumbled.

A Wakandan formation split.

Chitauri pursuing them found themselves facing stone where open ground had been.

Kaze’s smile disappeared.

He looked down.

The seam continued crawling.


There.

That was different.

Not an army.

Not a ship.

Not a transformation.

The world itself had just changed the rules between one footstep and the next.

Kaze crouched and touched the boundary.

Solid stone on one side.

Foreign debris on the other.

The line moved beneath his fingers.

He pulled his hand back.

“Okay.”


“What?”

Kaze stood.

His attention shifted between the moving seam, Carol tearing apart Sanctuary II, and the Gauntlet still traveling through the war below.

Three mysteries.

One battlefield.

His interest had choices now.

The Gauntlet won first.

Because everyone was willing to die for it.

Kaze rolled one shoulder, adjusted his grip on The Oblivion Tempest, and looked toward the figure carrying it.

“Imma see what that shit is.”


“Kaze.”

“What?”

“We don’t know these people.”

“I know.”

“You don’t know what that thing does.”

“I know.”


Kaze started walking.

That answer had apparently made the idea more attractive.


Above them, Sanctuary II began to die.

Ahead of them, the Infinity Stones changed hands again.

And for the first time in the Battle of Earth, someone who had absolutely nothing to do with either side had decided the Gauntlet was interesting enough to go look at personally.

⸻

PART 3

Kaze went after the Gauntlet.

Not because anyone had asked him to.

Quite the opposite, in fact.

Every person who understood what the bloody thing was would’ve preferred the unfamiliar white-eyed swordsman stay well away from it.

But Kaze had spent enough time watching the battlefield to learn one useful fact: men who ignored incoming fire to chase an object usually knew something about that object worth learning.

Peter Parker had it.

For the moment.

The lad swung over broken concrete with the Gauntlet clutched against his chest while Chitauri fire stitched the ground below him. He released the web, landed badly, rolled, came back up and found three Outriders already closing.

The first never reached him.

Kaze dropped between them.

His Air Forces struck the ground hard enough to fracture the slab beneath him.

One Outrider leapt.

Kaze caught it by the face.

The body kept going.

The neck did not.

He threw the corpse into the second hard enough to break both against a chunk of Avengers Compound wreckage.

The third stopped.

Kaze looked at it.

The creature screamed and attacked.

“Never mind.”

The Oblivion Tempest cleared his left hip in one right-handed draw.

One cut.

The Outrider came apart.

Peter froze with both hands around the Gauntlet.

Kaze looked at it.

“Lemme see that.”

“No.”

Kaze blinked.

“No?”

“Definitely no.”

“Why?”

Peter gestured frantically at the war surrounding them.

“Because everybody wants it!”

Kaze looked around.

“That’s why I wanna see it.”

“That doesn’t make it better!”

A Chitauri blast struck the concrete beside them.

Peter ducked.

Kaze turned.

Two gunners occupied a broken platform thirty yards away.

He swung once.

Wind compressed ahead of the Tempest.

The platform vanished behind an explosion of steel, debris and bodies.

Kaze looked back.

“What’s it do?”

Peter hesitated.

Then he saw Thanos.

The Titan had seen the Gauntlet too.

Steve Rogers moved to intercept him.

Thanos struck Steve aside.

Thor came next with Stormbreaker.

Thanos redirected the axe and kept coming.

Peter looked at Kaze.

Then Thanos.

Then the glove.

His options were becoming fewer by the second.

“Infinity Stones.”

Kaze waited.

“That don’t tell me shit.”

“Space, time, reality, power, mind and soul.”

Kaze’s gaze dropped to the six Stones.

“All that?”

“Yes.”

“In one glove.”

“Yes.”

Kaze stared for one beat longer.

“That’s fucken ridiculous.”

Peter pointed past him.

“THANOS.”

Kaze turned.

Thanos had closed half the distance.

Peter made his decision.

He threw the Gauntlet.

Kaze caught it.

The battlefield noticed.

Tony Stark noticed.

Steve noticed.

Thor noticed.

Doctor Strange noticed.

Carol Danvers, descending through the wreckage of Sanctuary II, noticed.

And Thanos changed from advancing to charging.

[Author-removed historical passage omitted.]

Across the battlefield, Thanos reached Kaze.

The Titan’s double-edged sword came down at his head.

Kaze moved half a step.

The blade split the ground.

Thanos reversed instantly.

Better than the Chitauri.

Faster.

More disciplined.

The Oblivion Tempest caught the second strike.

The impact cracked the concrete beneath both men.

Thanos pushed.

Kaze didn’t.

For a moment, the Titan appeared not to understand what he was feeling.

Then he did.

The smaller man simply wasn’t giving ground.

Thanos stared.

“Give me the Stones.”

Kaze glanced at the Gauntlet tucked under his other arm.

“So you’re Thanos.”

Thanos pulled his sword free and attacked again.

Kaze stepped inside the arc.

Caught his wrist.

Thanos punched with the other hand.

Kaze caught that too.

Both of the Titan’s arms stopped.

The Gauntlet remained in Kaze’s other arm against his side.

Thanos pulled.

Nothing happened.

Kaze’s interest deteriorated visibly.

“Everybody been doing all this over you?”

Thanos pulled.

Nothing.

Kaze’s interest deteriorated visibly.

“Why you want everybody dead?”

Thanos drove a knee upward.

Kaze shifted.

The knee missed.

Thanos answered with absolute conviction.

“This universe will be reborn grateful.”

Kaze stared.

“That’s your reason?”

Thanos tried to wrench one hand loose.

Kaze’s grip tightened.

Bone cracked.

Thanos grunted and kept reaching for the Gauntlet.

Kaze looked at Peter.

“This motherfucker stupid?”

Peter pointed at Thanos.

“He killed half the universe.”

Kaze looked back.

“Yeah, that don’t answer my question.”

Thanos surged forward again.

The Tempest moved.

One clean stroke.

No prolonged exchange.

No waiting for another speech.

Thanos’s head separated from his body.

The Titan’s momentum carried the body one final step.

Then both pieces hit the ground.

Kaze remained still.

He waited.

The battlefield roared around him.

Thanos did not rise.

Kaze nudged the body with one shoe.

Nothing.

“Dead.”

Peter stared at him.

Kaze looked back.

“What?”

“You just—”

“Yeah.”

Across the battlefield, Thanos’s death did not magically switch his army off.

What it did was worse for them.

It removed the mind all their most important decisions had been orbiting.

Some Chitauri continued attacking.

Outriders kept throwing themselves at anything alive.

Leviathans followed momentum and command already received.

But formations faltered.

Officers looked for orders that would not come.

The campaign to recover the Gauntlet became dozens of separate fights instead of one coherent drive.

Tony Stark landed near Kaze.

His helmet retracted.

“Okay.”

Kaze looked at him.

Tony pointed at the Gauntlet.

“That comes back to us.”

Kaze looked down at it.

“Kid already told me what it does.”

Peter shouted, “I told him what the Stones are!”

Tony stared at Peter.

“You told the stranger with the demonic sword about all six Infinity Stones?”

“He asked!”

Tony looked back at Kaze.

Kaze shrugged.

“Good kid.”

“Not helping.”

Bruce Banner’s voice carried from the shattered compound.

“I used them.”

Kaze turned.

Banner sat amid broken concrete with his ruined right arm stabilized against his body.

Kaze’s expression changed.

He walked closer.

Tony followed.

Steve arrived from the other side.

Kaze lifted the Gauntlet slightly.

“This did that?”

Banner looked at his arm.

“Yes.”

“One use?”

“One.”

Kaze looked back at the Stones.

“What’d you do?”

“Restored everyone Thanos erased.”

Kaze’s eyes moved across the battlefield.

Now the portals meant something different.

So did the reunions he’d barely registered while fighting.

People who had been dead were here.

Not copies.

Not soldiers pulled from somewhere else.

Returned.

The Gauntlet suddenly became more interesting.

And more dangerous.

Kaze stared at it long enough for Tony’s posture to tighten.

Then Steve held out a hand.

“Please.”

Kaze looked at Steve.

Then Tony.

“See? That’s how you ask.”

Tony closed his eyes.

Kaze handed Steve the Gauntlet.

Peter’s shoulders dropped so fast it looked painful.

Tony stared at Kaze.

“You just gave it back.”

Kaze looked at him.

“I wanted to know what it was.”

“And that’s it?”

“For now.”

Tony did not like those last two words.

Steve heard them too.

So did Strange.

Nobody attacked.

Nobody thanked him either.

That was sensible.

They knew too little.

Kaze didn’t mind.

Then Runeterra moved.

Not metaphorically.

The actual ground.

A dark seam raced underneath the battlefield.

Foreign concrete on one side.

Native stone on the other.

When the boundary passed through the crater containing Thanos, the crater vanished.

Head.

Body.

Blood.

Broken earth.

Gone with the section of geography that had held them.

Steve stepped toward the place instinctively.

“What happened?”

Kaze looked at the pale stone now standing where Thanos had died.

“Road shit.”

Tony turned.

“Road what?”

“The world’s been doing this.”

Strange’s full attention snapped onto him.

“Doing what?”

“Moving shit.”

Another seam opened.

A section of battlefield shifted sideways.

Not sliding.

One location stopped touching another.

A Runeterran road appeared between two broken parts of the compound.

The people on it screamed as Chitauri debris suddenly occupied the route ahead.

Part of the compound disappeared behind a wall of unfamiliar rock.

Wong opened a portal for several wounded fighters.

The golden ring formed.

Its far side flickered.

Wong shut it before anyone stepped through.

Strange tried another.

Short range.

Stable.

Farther.

The endpoint changed.

He killed the spell.

Tony saw his face.

“That’s bad.”

Strange looked across the fractured terrain.

“Distance isn’t the issue.”

Kaze said, “Short version.”

Strange looked at him.

“The world cannot reliably decide what is adjacent to what.”

Kaze nodded.

“Yeah. That.”

Tony stared.

“You already knew?”

“I knew roads were moving.”

“And this didn’t seem worth mentioning?”

Kaze looked around.

“To who?”

Tony opened his mouth.

Stopped.

There had been no shared authority.

Kaze had not been part of their army.


There was no time for that now.


The slab descended toward the Runeterran road.

He caught it.

The ground under his feet cratered.


People were still underneath.


Not yet.

Wrong angle.

Wrong moment.

“Move!”


A traveler stumbled.


A second piece of debris fell.


His body surged larger again.


It changed course.

Hit the road beside him.


Then he turned.

Every part of the motion was deliberate.

Hips.

Shoulders.

Footing.

He hurled the slab away from the road.

It crashed into an empty section of battlefield.


His breathing had changed.

The gamma in him answered strain with heat and strength, not mindlessness.

He looked .

The dog returned to base again, panting hard but alert.


“You good?”


“Good.”

That was when the road beneath them separated from the battlefield.


The section shifted perhaps thirty yards sideways.

The Avengers Compound was no longer directly beside them.

A different stretch of Runeterran terrain now occupied the gap.

Kaze saw.

Strange saw.

They both reached the same conclusion from different directions.

Ordinary evacuation routes could not be trusted.

Strange looked at Kaze’s sword.

“You can cut space.”

Kaze’s eyes narrowed.

“How you know?”

Peter raised one hand.

Kaze looked at him.

Peter lowered it.

Tony said, “Can you make something stable?”

Kaze looked at the changing terrain.

“Probably.”

Tony stared.

“Your confidence is inspiring.”

Kaze ignored him.

He selected a point across the broken road.

A place he could actually see.

The Tempest moved.

Space opened.

No circle.

No sparks.


Kaze picked up a broken Chitauri plate.

Threw it through.

It landed on the far side.

Stayed.

Kaze looked at Strange.

Strange looked at the opening.

A seam moved between the two locations.

Kaze’s cut remained.

That was enough.

Steve stepped toward it.

“Can people cross?”

Kaze pointed at the armor.

“That did.”

Tony looked at him.

“We are not using the wounded as test subjects.”

Kaze shrugged.

“Then you go.”

Tony stared.

Kaze pointed at the suit.

“You got armor.”

Tony looked at Strange.

Strange examined the cut without touching it.

“I see no immediate instability.”

Tony’s helmet closed.

“I hate everybody here.”

He stepped through.

Appeared .

Turned.

Walked back.

Helmet opened.

“Works.”

Kaze looked at Steve.

“There.”

Steve immediately started moving wounded people toward it.

No debate.

No ceremonial alliance.

A practical solution existed.

They used it.

Strange watched the route as stretchers, fighters and Runeterran travelers crossed.

The geography between its endpoints changed twice.

The scar did not.

Kaze left it open.

Not for charity.

Because a stable point in an unstable world was useful.

Other people benefiting from it did not make the usefulness less real.


“You helping.”

Kaze looked at him.

“I’m testing.”

“Helping.”

“Shut up.”


“He agrees.”

Kaze looked at the dog.

“You been real political today.”


The first stories about Kaze left the battlefield through that same scar.

Not speeches.

Witnesses.

Runeterran travelers who had watched the white-eyed swordsman kill the foreign army’s commander, then cut open a route through broken geography.


Nobody understood any of it.

Understanding was not necessary for testimony.

They left carrying what they had actually witnessed.

Rumor finally had bones.

Far from the main battle, a piece of Thanos’s war fell into water.

Catfish Billy reached it before anyone else.

The damaged Chitauri skiff floated half-submerged, one engine section burned open and a surviving soldier trying to pull itself free.

Billy’s six barbels mapped the movement.

The soldier saw him.

Raised its weapon.

Fired.

Billy opened his mouth.

The shot went inside.

So did the soldier.

So did the front half of the wreck.

One rapid swallow.

Inside CATFISH BAYOU, Chris was sitting in front of Red Dead Redemption 2 when a new containment chamber convulsed.

He paused the game.

Slowly turned.

“…Billy.”

Something hit the far side of the living membrane.

Chris got up.

Walked closer.

A Chitauri soldier rolled across the isolated zone, came up with its weapon and fired.

Energy struck the membrane.

The wall flexed.

Chris jumped back.

“HEY!”

The alien fired again.

“STOP FUCKEN SHOOTING MY WALL!”

Another blast.

Chris stared.

Then his eyes shifted away from the soldier.

To the weapon.

Then the wreckage behind it.

Metal.

Panels.

Wiring.

A broken control surface.

Unknown components.

His anger reorganized itself instantly.

“Oh.”

The Chitauri shouted.

Chris barely heard it.

He pressed closer to the membrane.

“Do that again.”

The alien fired.

Chris watched the gun cycle.

“Okay.”

Another shot.

Heat vented from one section.

Chris’s eyes narrowed.

“Okay.”

The Chitauri raised the weapon again.

Chris pointed.

“Don’t die.”

The alien obviously did not understand.

Chris turned his head upward.

“Billy!”

The bond answered with predatory satisfaction.

“Do not digest this motherfucker.”

Billy’s amusement rolled through him.

“I’m serious.”

Chris pointed at the wreck.

“And keep the ship.”

More amusement.

“ALL THE SHIT.”

Outside, Billy turned toward additional debris floating across the displaced water.

Inside, Chris watched another piece of alien metal arrive in an empty zone.

His face lit up.

“Billy.”

Another fragment.

A panel.

Bent armor.

Something with wiring attached.

Chris abandoned the television completely.

He began sorting what he could access.

Not building.

Not pretending to understand alien engineering.

Possession first.

KEEP.

PARTS.

PROJECT.

MAYBE EXPLODES.

The last pile stayed farthest from the lounge.

He held up a tiny unfamiliar fastener.

Stared at it.

“See, this the shit people throw away.”

The Chitauri screamed something from containment.

Chris pointed at it.

“You shut up. I’m working.”

The first KNOCKTURNAL salvage entered Chris’s hoard.

And because Chris remembered his piles, it would not vanish merely because the story looked elsewhere.

Back at the battlefield, the fighting had thinned.

Thanos was dead.

The Gauntlet was secured.

Tony Stark was alive.

That alone had changed an entire future none of them could yet see.

Kaze stood near the spatial scar while Strange examined the shifting horizon.

Then Kaze’s danger sense hit.

Hard.

He turned.


Banner felt something too, though not through any supernatural sense.

Recognition came later.

The ground beyond the shattered battlefield ruptured.

A body came through the displacement and hit Runeterra hard enough to throw earth in every direction.

The crater settled.

A huge green hand reached over the edge.

Bruce Banner went still.

The figure pulled himself up.

Different face.

Different posture.

Same impossible familiarity.

Savage Hulk.

Not Professor Hulk.

Not Banner transformed again.

Another Hulk.

Displaced whole.

Confused.

Already angry.

He looked across the battlefield.

At Bruce.


At Kaze.

Banner stared at him.

Tony looked at Banner.

Then the newcomer.

Then back.

“No.”

Banner said quietly, “That’s me.”

Tony pointed at Banner.

“You’re you.”

Banner looked sick.

“That’s who I used to be.”


His confusion found something simple enough to grab.

Another enormous green thing.

Hulk pointed.

“YOU HULK?”


Then slowly toward Kaze.

Kaze’s lips were already twitching.

“Don’t.”

Kaze looked away.


“Don’t fucken laugh.”

Kaze failed.

Savage Hulk roared.


Hulk charged.


“Back.”


Enough.


Compact guard.

Hulk swung.


The fist passed close enough to move his hair.


Short hook.

No huge windup.

The impact blew dust outward.

Hulk moved half a step.

Stopped.

Looked down.


His anger changed.

Not less.

Interested.

“HULK LIKE GREEN MAN.”


“Goddammit.”

Hulk swung again.


Body shot.

Hulk’s elbow came down.


The impact drove him knee-deep into broken ground.

Kaze stopped laughing.


Hulk left his feet.

Landed.

Rolled.

Stood immediately.

Stronger anger now.

His strength rose with it.


He adjusted.

Less trading.

More angle.

Hulk rushed.


Hook to the body.

Pivot.


The blow launched the much larger man through a broken section of foreign wall.


His body surged.

Golden energy flared.

This time he did not stop at Giant.


Nearly seven feet of transformed canine mass drove across the gap.


The collision moved the green giant just enough.

Hulk turned.

Saw the enormous glowing dog.

Confusion returned.


Hulk stared.

For one absurd second, neither moved.


Blood at one corner of his mouth.

Already closing damage beneath green skin.


Protector Fury hit him like fuel.

“HEY.”

Hulk turned.


Peek-a-boo guard tight.

He slipped inside Hulk’s first swing.

Left hook.

Right to the body.

Another left.

Uppercut.


Hulk absorbed them.

Each one angered him.

Each one strengthened the next answer.


The ground cracked beneath both.

For the first time, Savage Hulk met something in front of him that did not immediately yield.


Kaze stepped closer.


“Don’t.”

Kaze stopped.


“This one mine.”

Kaze looked at Hulk.


A grin returned.

“Aight.”


Banner watched his past self fighting a stranger who looked more like Hulk than Banner did now.

Strange watched the displacement crater.

Steve watched the wounded.

Tony watched all of them and realized that Thanos’s death had not ended anything.

It had merely freed the battlefield for the next problem.


The collision shook the road.

The fight continued.

# KAZE — Integrated Season 2 and Season 3 Blueprint

6 October 2026 UTC. Current story: **paused after X.2; X.3 is next.**

## Authority and how to use this document

This is the **single current committed prospective outline** authorized by “esketit qapx + update google drive.” It replaces competing future-card arrangements, not delivered history, accepted character choices or the research ledgers. The earlier Season 2 Story Plan and the proposed arrangement in the storytelling research document are retained as historical planning evidence.

Normal plot construction, sequence, local settings, jobs, character decisions and a working calendar are committed here as prospective authoring choices. They are not events already played. S37 commits this configuration; PLAY has not occurred during the commit. No previous source audit is restarted or newly declared perfect.

**Latest user addendum integrated:** Viktor's power and Singed's science now have a specific, bounded joint containment design. Counterplay remains real; Warwick's own limited choice gives the release a reason even when the Sins win the rescue. The full-kit application pass applies to every Sin, including the signature techniques specifically requested.

This is a plot-bearing planning reference. It does **not** disclose unannounced future victims, fatal methods, responsible killers, casualty totals or exact fatal episodes. The user's selected Viktor endpoint is preserved, without locating its execution. The Sins and Jesse remain protected, and Heisenberg remains active beyond Season 2. These constraints do not make injuries, setbacks or lost relationships unreal.

### Selected capacity and clocks

- **Season 2:** nineteen remaining cards, X.3 and XI.1–XVI.3; D0–D120 measured forward from X.2.
- **First arrival:** a chosen 28-day capitalward passage; Noxian arrival D148 in this working schedule. This is not a supernatural travel restriction. Once valid endpoints are available, the gang can use Kaze's actual portals; Matt's later transfers do.
- **Season 3:** eight acts of three cards, **24 working episodes**, over R0–R126, where R0=D148. This is the author's capacity estimate for the selected ensemble, not a prior user-locked episode count.
- Acts in the Season 3 section restart at I. They are not a new “Season 4.”
- A card may contain a short signaled interval. No number of episode cuts substitutes for the events needed to establish a choice or a capability.

## The connection between the seasons

Season 2 asks what people will justify to keep control when care, policing, family and livelihood become bargaining tools. Season 3 asks the user's central question: **when a place rewards everything dangerous about you, what stops you becoming worse?**

The connection is material, not just thematic:

| Season 2 action | Changed situation | Season 3 consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Heisenberg gains a repeatable Bilgewater commercial foothold. | Foreign clients can request goods through a functioning, limited intermediary. | A Noxian private works becomes a customer; its failures cause delayed, real losses in Zaun. |
| The notebook fragment is stolen, used and investigated. | Heisenberg retains dangerous paper but loses his compromised official identity-information channel. | His need for trustworthy humans grows. Matt's later case and Ryuk's observer strand continue without a new notebook reset. |
| Frank chooses enhancement, tests the gang's freedom to refuse, and eventually joins. | The gang gains an equal whose objections can change its actions. | A profitable job involving transported people creates an internal decision, not automatic obedience to a new commander. |
| Black Rose and Swain's separate observers learn limited facts. | Different Noxian interests have reasons to investigate, court or oppose the newcomers. | Offers conflict. No one faction secretly planned every arrival or owns every subplot. |
| The Sins leave assets and a claim in Zaun. | Departure is not abandonment of their history, nor an intact occupied base guarded by absent superhumans. | A continuing reason to care about Zaun survives their independent Noxian life. |

The Sins remain a dangerous, selectively loyal, poverty-rooted gang. Helping someone may be a choice; humanitarian service is not their job description. They choose trouble, take profit and defend their own. Their refusal to become somebody else's property does not make them morally innocent.

# Season 2 — operative sequence

The following nineteen cards are one sequence. The day and concrete-object lines resolve the abbreviations in each card; they are not optional parallel versions. Locations described by function are original branch staging within the researched setting, not claims of newly discovered official atlas sites. Unnamed workers, soldiers and handlers remain supported local roles, not additions to the locked named crossover roster.


### X.3 — The visit already promised

**Day: D0. Selected staging:** Billy's interior meeting with Vi and Jinx is the actual promised visit. He grants a request to end the visit and leave, establishing one specific kept promise. No treatment gives Singed access to Warwick yet.


- **Immediate objective:** Billy and the gang host the sisters' visit without surrendering Warwick; Vi and Jinx seek an identifiable response from their father, not an instant cure.
- **Causal predecessor:** X.2 ends with the explicit request and agreement. The audience has just seen public versions of Jinx that conflict with the person waiting outside Billy.
- **Choice and change:** Recognition makes doing nothing harder for the sisters. Billy agrees to a bounded treatment inquiry while retaining custody. He makes and honors one specific narrow promise: the sisters may end their visit and leave when they ask. His later promise-keeping cannot be invented only at the hostage exchange. The gang now has an obligation it chose, and the sisters possess a concrete reason to approach care rather than a vague hope supplied by the narrator.
- **Information:** Those present learn only the recognition and instability they witness. Nobody outside learns Warwick's location until the inquiry later carries that information. Show Billy's consent to each access change.
- **Brief secondary:** A new observed problem with Viktor's already-altered body makes Jayce and Viktor choose the next limited step involving the still-isolated Hexcore. Preserve Sky's already-delivered fate and consequences; do not replay them.
- **Carry forward:** A specific request for care, Warwick still inside Billy, and Walter's denied-control grievance. Give the audience the plain relationship: the dangerous body is Vi and Jinx's father.

### XI.1 — The price of clearing a route

**Day: D2. Selected staging:** The contested place is a lower-city service passage feeding the existing medicine/water-point route. Caitlyn's bounded Grey operation closes it; the Sins force an exit for their own group and the known medicine carrier. Access stays disrupted after they leave.


- **Immediate objective:** The Sins keep a route and possessions they actually care about usable as an Enforcer Grey operation reaches an inhabited district. Their purpose is not to become Caitlyn's rescue unit.
- **Causal predecessor:** Caitlyn's measured X.1 response and X.2 injuries fail to resolve a concrete armed obstruction or investigative limit. Ambessa offers escalation that answers that limit while increasing her leverage.
- **Choice and change:** Caitlyn personally chooses a bounded Grey operation after securing access and a real team. The Sins refuse terms that hand their autonomy to the authorities. They get themselves and particular allies through, but the raid alters ordinary access, public trust and shelter needs rather than being erased by one powerful gang action.
- **Information:** Caitlyn knows her stated operational target, not every casualty or later recruitment effect. The gang sees local conduct and resulting harm, not her private briefing. Ambessa receives actual reports. Frank encounters identifiable endangered people and culpable actions through his existing independent work.
- **Brief secondary:** Ambessa's first approach to Frank offers relevant verified information or access; usefulness is not acceptance. Her recruiting interest stays grounded in what she has learned about him.
- **Carry forward:** The same people and goods displaced here appear in XI.2. Use the existing bridge porter and medicine carrier rather than inventing an anonymous new crowd for each consequence. The gang's material success and the wider damage can coexist.

### XI.2 — The people everyone needs

**Day: D4. Selected staging:** At the existing water-point work, damaged filtration and displaced patients make the same service passage matter again. Heimer and Ekko restore a usable intake; Viktor genuinely relieves one recurring sufferer. The success is not secretly fabricated.


- **Immediate objective:** The gang gets useful supplies or shelter access restored during the practical emergency the raid worsened. Heimer and Ekko contribute to that action through existing water-point/community work.
- **Causal predecessor:** XI.1 changes demand and safe access. X.3 establishes the next Viktor step; it has not happened merely because the outline needs a healer.
- **Choice and change:** An intervention actually works. Viktor's new capacity gives genuine relief to a recurring affected person. Jesse helps someone he knows. The Sins see why people would seek this place; no narrator retrospectively calls all early care fraudulent.
- **Information:** Heimer and Ekko observe a concrete technical abnormality while addressing immediate need. Viktor knows his experience, not the eventual network's full properties. Jesse knows the relieved person's response, not an invisible universal cure.
- **Brief secondary:** Frank sees the difference between courage and what his current body can survive. He asks a practical question about Chris's existing competence and resources. This begins an independent, voluntary decision, not an offer that buys membership.
- **Carry forward:** Useful care gains trust; a specific worker/customer's dependence changes. The sisters have a credible treatment lead. Caitlyn has witnesses to the consequences of her choice.

### XI.3 — Ownership, not a better wage

**Day: D7. Selected staging:** Walter rejects shared control of an existing Shimmer processing operation and takes controlling terms. Jesse refuses responsibility for coerced recruitment. 47 accepts paid recurring protection/investigative work with explicit limits. The narrative name changes to Heisenberg here.


- **Immediate objective:** Walter secures a functioning Shimmer operation on terms he controls; the Sins refuse to make their route its compulsory distribution service.

- **Choice and change:** A workable shared arrangement is available. He chooses coercive ownership instead, acquires actual organizational capacity and accepts its cost. Narration changes to **Heisenberg** at this local transition. A surviving rival, including Renata where her actual interests touch the transaction, retains resources and responds.

Kaze reads the practical control claim; Daniel distinguishes the offer's omitted conditions without receiving private knowledge.

### XII.1 — Asking for help exposes a secret

**Day: D14. Selected staging:** The agreed care inquiry occurs at the commune's courtyard treatment bay. Billy permits observation and discussion only; the one Warwick body remains inside him. The same place becomes familiar . Frank consents to Chris's independent project.


- **Immediate objective:** The sisters and gang test whether care can reach Warwick without transferring his custody.

**Day: D28. Selected staging:** The disputed access is the lower-city service passage, now subject to a Noxian inspection post. **Day: D49. Selected staging:** A commune recipient asks to leave a movement-regulation treatment. Viktor delays ending it to finish what he considers beneficial, then releases the person. **Day: D52. Selected staging:** The first retaliatory target is the treatment annex's troop staging and transport support used in the seizure. ### XIV.1 — Information is a person

**Day: D63. Selected staging:** The pursuit centers on the compromised evidence handler and the worker-information access that handler sold. Jesse refuses to supply a requested identification of a protected witness; Matt gets the witness out. Frank rejects selling that person back as leverage. - **Choice and change:** Jesse withholds or diverts a specific piece of information he actually controls; Matt gets a person safely through the immediate danger; Frank rejects treating the witness as a disposable bargaining object. The gang accepts an inconvenient limit or loses an available profit to preserve the person. This is a concrete membership test, not an announcement that the Sins have become heroes.
- **Information:** Matt's senses help him follow the encounter, not read unknown supernatural rules. The protected source supplies an actual name, access record or transaction fact to investigators. 47 pursues his contracted objective but cannot make the entire institution forget the missing item. An opposed objective does not require an evenly matched Frank–47 duel.
- **Brief secondary:** Ryuk observes how Heisenberg and Jesse differ over the human meaning of information. A short question or refusal to supply a missing answer forces Walter to rely on, and answer to, his human relationships. No intelligence-service Ryuk.

**Day: D70. Selected staging:** The sisters seek entry to the Warwick treatment annex after its support was damaged. The gang targets its remaining coercive transfer equipment. - **Immediate objective:** The sisters' effort to reach Warwick collides with the Sins' continuing retaliation at a site or access route established by the prior custody change.
- **Causal predecessor:** XIII.2 altered Warwick's custody; XIII.3 removed a capability; XIV.1 produced limited usable information. Each faction arrives for its own goal.
- **Choice and change:** The gang cannot satisfy its grievance by ignoring the people the sisters and community are trying to preserve. A practical decision distinguishes a person from a process. The operation yields a real fact about dependence/coercion and changes the safe intervention available later, without solving the whole network.
- **Information:** Heimer, Ekko and Jayce each compare a distinct observation. None gains the others' knowledge without contact. Keep the already-earned four-second Z-Drive limit; no reinvention or notebook reversal. Mel's information has its own channel and does not explain unrelated crime.
- **Brief secondary:** The upgraded fighters encounter the consequence of earlier use or damage. The scene uses that state, not an arbitrary new battery failure to equalize opponents.

**Day: D84. Selected staging:** Heisenberg knowingly pays for replacement laboratory access protecting Singed's useful work after learning why the Sins are pursuing it. The gang learns the new support through observed material, money or access, not a narrator cutaway it could not hear. 47 reports a real limitation rather than magically eliminating opposition.

A bounded raven observation records a relevant public/visible development; Swain receives only what that channel actually transmits.

**Day: D98. Selected staging:** The service passage becomes the disputed route out of the growing coercive area. Firelights refuse to abandon their community to defend the gang's property; the Sins instead defend the passage for a bounded crossing that also protects their stake. Track its remaining usable surface without inventing further pieces. The main notebook and watch remain under the evidence chain. ### XV.3 — Choose what the next victory is for

**Day: D100. Selected staging:** The immediate target is an occupation mobilization yard supplying the already contested route. The Sins and their limited partners remove its usable transport stores, opening the next access without defeating every Noxian unit. ### XVI.1 — Break a capacity, not a symbol

**Day: D101. Selected staging:** The occupation loses the service-passage command and resupply position that previous damage made vulnerable. - **Choice and change:** Military control loses something it cannot restore within the remaining confrontation. A separate criminal achievement remains lost to Heisenberg. His established organization and Bilgewater transaction sustain continuation without erasing those losses.
- **Information:** Mel acts on the evidence and power she has actually developed in her own conflict. Caitlyn's changed alignment with authority follows accountability, not retroactive innocence. Swain and the Black Rose receive different incomplete information; neither controls the other by outline convenience.
- **Brief secondary:** Preserve the meaning of Frank's voluntary gang membership and Jesse's planned independent future. Do not use aftermath to bind either to Heisenberg. No future casualty identities are supplied here.
- **Carry forward:** Specific military, criminal and relationship conditions change. No single fallen commander or destroyed building settles the full city.

### XVI.2 — The coercive process

**Day: D102. Selected staging:** The confrontation stops the already established forced-conversion/treatment intake and denies its immediate means of expansion, with Jayce/Viktor and the sisters' choices remaining consequential. No universal anti-hive switch, all-patient death or universal cure is assumed. The selected personal endpoint is not allocated to an exact fatal scene in this reader-facing plan.


- **Immediate objective:** Confront the culminating process this branch actually established, using the limits and available interventions the characters discovered.
- **Causal predecessor:** Real early help, refusals overridden, the Warwick bargain, retaliation losses and the scientific inquiry produced this version of the threat. Do not copy a source finale whose prerequisites do not exist here.
- **Choice and change:** Physical action earns the opportunity for a decisive human choice. Native protagonists, especially the sisters and Viktor/Jayce's relationship, remain consequential; the crossover cast does not replace every source character's function. Account separately for connected people and institutions after any personal endpoint.
- **Information:** No borrowed certainty from alternate futures unless the branch enacted that knowledge route. Effects on patients are observed and addressed individually as supported; there is no assumed automatic mass death or instant universal cure.
- **Brief secondary:** Only what directly changes the confrontation; no unrelated catch-up roll call.
- **Carry forward:** The immediate coercive crisis reaches its substantive resolution. The selected Viktor endpoint is retained separately without locating its method, responsible actor or exact episode in this card.

### XVI.3 — The city they are leaving

**Day: D120. Selected staging:** The gang retains its own workshop/store and recoverable assets under a bounded local caretaking arrangement using an existing trusted contact; this is not a promise to escort Heisenberg. No absent Sin supplies remote security. They choose Noxian trouble, Jesse chooses his own passage, and Matt leaves for the Noxian lead early enough to arrive by R0.


- **Immediate objective:** Settle the gang's immediate obligations and usable Zaun stake before it chooses to travel.
- **Causal predecessor:** The preceding operations changed the city; leaving now follows a decision about what the gang wants next, not receipt of an assigned mercenary mission.
- **Choice and change:** Confirm the already-earned criminal dominance, Bilgewater foothold, genuine cure, voluntary membership and available upgrades. Distinguish Heisenberg's criminal influence from formal sovereignty over Zaun or command of independent factions. The Sins retain assets and a claim worth returning to without suddenly becoming landlords responsible for all Zaun.
- **Information:** Specific Black Rose/raven/occupation-backer evidence creates a plausible continuing Noxian interest. The gang can pursue that interest while also wanting challenges, profit and trouble on its own terms. They do not need every Noxian secret to leave. Jesse chooses Noxus for his own reasons; Matt's case requires actual travel and contacts between regions.
- **Brief secondary:** Zaun's ongoing strand contains Heisenberg, his organization and independent opposition. Ryuk's continuing interest concerns what people choose around the notebook; his location and obligations remain accountable, not a permanent narrator pass.
- **Carry forward:** Clear relative departure date, injuries/recovery, equipment, property custody, obligations and people who know the departure. Deadpool's brief takeover belongs at the Season 3 opening; it does not prematurely advance PLAY here.


## Viktor's actual endgame and the scientific counter-story

The threat is **expanding compulsory incorporation**, not simply a spooky commune. Viktor comes to believe that independent will perpetuates suffering. His selected concrete objective is to connect successive groups through an organized conversion intake, use those linked bodies to bring in more people, and make refusal progressively impossible across the two cities.

This branch does not silently inherit a fully operational source-finale Hexgate system. Its immediate technical center is **the already-established Hexcore and the commune treatment network**, brought into a Noxian-supported conversion chamber in the existing treatment annex. The chamber and its limited tests must be shown as new development over the middle weeks, not unveiled as an unexplained finale machine. Later citywide expansion is the dangerous next step being attempted, not a capability already assumed to cover every inhabitant.

| Card | Observable development and decision | What another strand must do differently |
| --- | --- | --- |
| X.3–XI.2 | Viktor's altered body develops a concrete control/stability problem. He asks Jayce for limited re-contact with the isolated Hexcore; the attempt opens real healing capacity as well as a dependence he does not yet understand. | Jayce owns responsibility for permitting the experiment. Heimer/Ekko encounter its effects through their practical community work, not future knowledge. |
| XII.1–XII.3 || The gang has reason to seek help and later reason to recognize betrayal. The apparatus is a particular invention, not proof of universal domination. |
| XIII.2–XIV.2 | Only after Warwick exits Billy does Singed obtain an actual opportunity to collect and study regenerative tissue at the treatment bay; show the contact or recovery of material rather than assume custody grants a sample. He proposes using its regenerative properties to stabilize bodies undergoing Viktor's more demanding changes. Viktor accepts the use of another person's body for his project. | Warwick's release produces a real scientific opportunity and a new ethical violation; he is not an unlimited ingredient cupboard. The sisters' demand for their father conflicts with the project. |
| XIV.2 | At the annex, one observed downstream interruption leaves a linked patient alive but unable to coordinate independently until the immediate connection is restored. The physical healing remains visible. Heimer and Ekko record the difference; Jayce connects the result to the Hexcore work he actually knows. | A blanket attack on all linked people is neither necessary nor justified. A safe result for this one patient is evidence to investigate, not proof that every body can be disconnected identically. |
| XIV.3–XV.1 | With Singed's bounded regenerative support, Viktor begins the chamber's small-scale intake and chooses to override refusal rather than stop. Military custody supplies access to unwilling people; Ambessa expects useful controlled troops. | Singed's laboratory losses and replacement patronage now affect the project's pace. The Firelights and Jinxers contest the route carrying people toward it. Ambessa and Viktor have overlapping but incompatible goals. |
| XV.3–XVI.2 | Viktor prepares a larger intake and dispatch of linked bodies beyond the commune. Ambessa wants military control; Viktor wants an end to the very independent ambition she relies on. The announced next transfer provides the immediate clock. | The Sins attack access and coercive capacity; native protagonists handle relationships and facts they uniquely possess. The objective is to stop this intake/expansion and the agency erasure behind it, not win a generic army fight. |

**Three distinct climax tasks:** stop the movement of unwilling people into the annex; disable the active chamber's expansion capacity without treating connected patients as disposable machinery; confront Viktor's continuing coercive choice. One smashed door or personal defeat does not automatically complete all three. Neither Kaze's available decisive attacks nor the native characters' knowledge may be withheld to manufacture a long battle.

Jayce's role is not “arrive with the right gun”: he helped permit the step that created this crisis and must confront the friend whose original wish he understood. Heimer is not a universal cure machine: his observations prevent a harmful indiscriminate intervention and make one bounded alternative testable. Ekko uses his already-earned four-second Z-Drive in a locally consequential action; it does not reverse a distant notebook entry or supply an unseen alternate-future biography.

Viktor's user-selected death remains a required destination, without publishing its method, actor or exact fatal episode. Recovery, dependence and personal outcomes of linked people are separately accounted for; there is no automatic mass death or universal restoration following his endpoint.

## Mel, the Black Rose and Caitlyn: a short consequential subplot

**The Rose's goal:** obtain leverage over Mel's unusual power and Ambessa's private military research through a controlled offer. It does not own Swain, the evidence handler or Heisenberg.

1. **XII.2 — the approach is also an attack.** A seemingly private contact about Medarda family security draws Mel into a Black Rose illusion/binding. Its supposed reassurance comes with pressure to turn against her mother's chosen research arrangement. Mel's existing/awakening defensive capacity becomes a deliberate act of resistance here; do not replay a first awakening if the delivered record already contains it.
2. **The specific clue:** the contact provides a copy of a real transfer authorization moving detained people and confiscated Hextech equipment into the Noxian-supported treatment annex under Medarda military protection. Its seal, unit and destination are testable. An illusion's assertion is not itself proof, and the Rose has selected this truth because it serves its own aim.
3. **XIV.3 — independent corroboration and choice.** Mel compares the copy against the real movement order and Caitlyn's records. She learns that the annex's detention/research purpose exceeds the Enforcers' stated security remit. She gives Caitlyn the corroborated order while withholding cooperation with the Rose's demand that she surrender her own judgment or become its instrument.
4. **XV.1 — a command changes.** Caitlyn uses that order, her own fragment investigation and the witnessed custody abuses to withdraw the Enforcer contribution to the next annex transfer. Ambessa has to replace that contribution with her own actual troops, reducing forces available elsewhere. Caitlyn remains accountable for earlier choices; new opposition does not erase the Grey.
5. **XVI.1 — power has an enacted use.** Mel uses the defensive capacity already developed in her Rose encounter to preserve the people and evidence during the contested withdrawal. It buys a local opportunity; it does not suppress all Noxian weapons or reveal every secret. Her refusal of both parties' total claim leaves Ambessa politically exposed and the Rose with less control than it expected.

This subplot's evidence concerns that specific transfer and family/military relationship. It does not solve the Death Note investigation, reveal every Noxian champion, or give the gang Mel's private knowledge. A bounded Rose contact and a separate raven observation provide different bridges into Season 3.


# Central bargains and body-state rules

# Shared calendar and travel ledger

## 1. Preserve what is actually known

| Anchor | Established evidence | Permitted conclusion |
|---|---|---|
| Season 2 opening, **E0** | Selected newcomer set Elsewhered around this opening under their separately selected source snapshots. | Arrival clustering is accepted; exact shared day, place and acquaintance are not. |
| VI.3 | Uploaded HISTORY evidence records seventeen lived days of workshop rebuilding and research after earlier events; its preserved clock explicitly says prior research/travel intervals also exist. | The seventeen-day segment occurred. It is not the whole season and is not new time added now. |
| X.1 into X.2 | Delivered X.1 begins in evening light and ends as lamps brighten; X.2 calls its action “tonight.” Neither supplies a total season length. | Treat X.3's promised visit as the same immediate continuity, not the far side of a new unannounced timeskip. |
| X.2 endpoint, **D0** | Current checkpoint and story plan consistently identify this cursor. | Define D0 as the end of X.2 for future scheduling. Do not manufacture a calendar date for it. |
| Earlier elapsed time, **Δ = D0 − E0** | Exact total remains unknown. It includes the recorded seventeen-day segment and other elapsed events. | Keep Δ symbolic. At least the known seventeen-day segment lies inside it; do not make Δ equal seventeen days or claim a precise month total. |

**Temporal rule:** X.2 stays where it is. Future D-days are measured forward from it. Native events are ordered by prerequisites and this branch's selected history, not by publication year, a guessed Noxian calendar year or the official show's episode number.

## 2. Selected working runway for integration: 120 future days

Use **D0–D120** for the remaining city-season development and departure. This is roughly four future months, not a retcon saying the earlier ten acts happened within four months. A future episode may cover hours, then cut across a clearly signaled interval. The camera need not spend that interval in laboratories or meetings.

Use these as target anchors for the nineteen committed prospective cards in this blueprint. A gap means continuing life and work, not guaranteed acquisition: decisive consent, breakthroughs, bargains, betrayals and organizational gains still occur in scenes.

| Working card | Proposed story day | Time-dependent work it accommodates |
|---|---:|---|
| X.3 | D0 | Immediate sisters/Warwick visit; no delayed fulfillment of X.2's promise. |
| XI.1 | D2 | Grey operation and its immediate human consequences. |
| XI.2 | D4 | Relief response and first meaningful new healing; Frank observes the threat he must decide how to meet. |
| XI.3 | D7 | Heisenberg's coercive foothold and 47's recurring employment become choices, not already-completed four-month achievements. |
| XII.1 | D14 | Frank independently consents to Chris's work; Warwick care inquiry generates a limited information leak; Bilgewater groundwork can begin. |
| XII.2 | D28 | Occupation and communal dependence have observable growth; enhancement development and bounded tests have had time; international dispatch can now be in transit. |
| XII.3 | D49 | Seven weeks of changing conditions precede Viktor's observable boundary crossing; paid scientific contact begins the cancer-treatment route without automatic Singed allegiance. |
| XIII.1 | D50 |capture mechanics must independently pass, regardless of the calendar.|
| XIII.2 | D50 | Same minutes-long incident as XIII.1; no overnight hostage delay. |
| XIII.3 | D52 | Immediate retaliation and its concrete consequences; already-tested upgrades may matter. |
| XIV.1 | D63 | A pursuit follows actual consequences; time for evidence, injuries and organizational adaptation does not erase the active grievance. |
| XIV.2 | D70 | Scientific/family intervention follows accumulated observations rather than instant omniscient diagnosis. |
| XIV.3 | D84 | Singed patronage conflict and a real Bilgewater commercial result; enough time for a voyage and an ordinary return report. |
| XV.1 | D98 | Genuine cancer cure is confirmed through the branch's established fictional diagnostic method before a later discretionary expansion choice. |
| XV.2 | D99 | Final custody/information operation inherits positions and damage. |
| XV.3 | D100 | Rapid escalation; no convenient recovery/reset between late confrontations. |
| XVI.1 | D101 | Connected late operations retain geography, resource use and injuries. |
| XVI.2 | D102 | Culminating confrontation; no new result or method specified by this calendar. |
| XVI.3 | D120 | Concentrated aftermath and departure: eighteen days for practical obligations/recovery where each actual injury permits it, not universal healing or a new empire supplied by montage. |

If the integrated outline changes the placement of one operation, preserve **causal intervals**, not these labels at any cost.

### Milestone checks

- **Frank:** D14 consent; D14–42 Chris-led biological/cybernetic work, supplied by existing capabilities/resources and enacted access; D42–49 bounded testing and energy/maintenance arrangements; package usable by D52. This does not prescribe a real medical process or create a new specialist. Chris alone remains developer.
- **47:** recurring terms at D7; package pathway/access established during D14–28; development/testing through D49; independent maintenance access demonstrated before first consequential reliance. Equal broad completion time does not mean the two projects share a lab, source, backdoor or identical design.
- **Heisenberg's cure:** first paid treatment contact by D49; fictional diagnosis, intervention and follow-up between D49 and D98; genuine cure confirmed at D98, with accepted longevity/mental-enhancement choices treated separately. This seven-week interval is an author-chosen science-fiction runway, not a claim that real oncology can certify a cure in that time. No newly invented recurrence is required to begin assessment.
- **Empire:** local gains start at D7; a first Bilgewater counterparty/dispatch is planted by D14–28; goods, responses and reinvestment occupy actual later weeks. Zaun dominance is demonstrated through several retained gains and surviving competition before D120. Dominance is not formal government or control of every faction.
- **Viktor:** D4 genuine healing, several weeks of social dependence, an observable boundary at D49, then consequences through D102. Do not portray every early patient as secretly dead from the outset, or allow a timeskip to stand in for his ethical decision.
- **Hostage grievance:** D50–52 is tightly linked. Later elapsed intervals occur after immediate retaliatory action

## 3. Route assumptions: concrete but fictional

The existing geography audit remains the evidence owner. Its G2–G3 historical Medarda trade chart supplies **18 days Piltover–Bilgewater**, **19 days on a Noxus route**, and **14 days to Basilich**. These are old trade benchmarks, not a scale for the modern atlas or proof of a ship docking at the Immortal Bastion. The audit supplies no verified universal Bilgewater–Noxus duration.

**Commercial production assumption:** normal merchant/passage ships, conventional capitalward final approach, normal preparation and access delays. These allowances govern travelers or goods actually using those methods. They do not disable Kaze's current Portals, saved endpoints, a legitimately established communication link or another earned faster method. The destination port remains a scene-design choice; the following allowances deliberately include getting from the actual embarkation point to the destination city, not only open-water speed.

| Route | Default production allowance | Normal planning window | Status and constraints |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Piltover/Zaun → Noxian capital | **28 days** | **24–32 days** | Fictional route allowance, loosely compatible with the historical 19-day Noxus benchmark plus actual port/capital approach and handling. Not a source-certified modern timetable. |
| Noxian capital → Piltover/Zaun | **28 days** | **24–32 days** | Same planning default for consistency, not proof winds/current are symmetric. A shown disruption can lengthen it. |
| Piltover/Zaun → Bilgewater | **22 days** | **18–26 days** | Fictional door-to-destination allowance using the 18-day historical trade figure as comparison, with handling/access variation. |
| Bilgewater → Piltover/Zaun | **22 days** | **18–26 days** | Same default; port security, ship availability and weather still apply. |
